# Design authority and value lineage

Registry revision: 1348 · Audit date: 2026-11-26 (America/Los_Angeles)

## Design authority

[D-GOV](#d-gov) covers the current registry revision and all sections. V-03 → GOV-01 → GOV-02 through E-11/E-12 makes each delegated scope traceable to its human-confirmed charter value. H-05 establishes the baseline. H-388/H-391/H-403/H-411/H-412 govern evidence, presentation and audit delivery. [Editorial continuity](../CHANGELOG.md#k-hygiene-index-20261123) · [Review routing and record location](../CHANGELOG.md#k-review-routing-20261126) · [Companion guide coverage](../CHANGELOG.md#k-composed-operation-retention-20261126) · [EPS research scope](../CHANGELOG.md#h-eps-library-direction-20261126)

The scope table gives the confirmed boundary, exclusions and open questions for each contract. Assess changes against its value and dependency edges, reverse links and original human evidence. The linked design owns detailed acceptance. Human confirmation, agent continuity and implementation coverage have distinct meanings. [Charter](RegionalSemanticPlatformCharter.md) · [Project rules](../AGENTS.md)

## 1. Audit and reading rules

1. Start with the relevant **document scope**, its named relationships and directly needed contracts. Read the charter when changing a design; unchanged qualified scope permits the local-contract shortcut in AGENTS.
2. **Confirmed** covers the stated proposition/revision/scope. **Continued** is an agent's contract-preserving assessment from that human baseline. **Pending-human** and **needs-reconfirmation** do not authorize implementation; **superseded/rejected** scopes are historical. Direction, concept, whole document and implementation acceptance are distinct.
3. Use one entry per independently decidable contract scope. The linked product document owns detailed semantics, non-goals and acceptance; this registry owns qualification and evidence. A routine implementation checkpoint needs no new concept, value edge or repeated status paragraph. Record coherent maintenance in one brief continuity event.
4. Nodes name topics. `derives-from` explains value; `depends-on` identifies prerequisites; `constrains` bounds behavior. Read each edge's scope/status: an ID, dependency or ancestor approval grants no additional authority. Apply current scopes and supersession before historical revision wording. Candidate value paths stay candidate even when associated behavior is confirmed.
5. For impact review, find both endpoints in the relationship table and use their contract links; follow value descendants and dependencies separately. These links route review, not new delegation. Preserve unaffected qualified scope; escalate actual evidence gaps, contradictions or uncovered material choices.
6. H-386 requires topic-based human discussion and eventual whole-file approval of new designs. Ask for concrete decisions, not internal IDs; do not reopen settled preferences. Build/test/toolchain permissions remain independent.
7. Human history is append-only in Git: preserve the actual source, statement, time uncertainty, scope and exclusions. Corrections/withdrawals get new events. Read original evidence when exact scope matters; an index or agent summary cannot replace confirmation. The [changelog evidence index](../CHANGELOG.md#authority-evidence-index) provides targeted retrieval.
8. [H-IMPLEMENTATION-FIDELITY-20261117](../CHANGELOG.md#h-implementation-fidelity-20261117) governs implementation work through [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#semantic-fidelity-and-implementation-obstacles): discuss any necessary semantic compromise at the design level before introducing it. Existing-code conflicts require disclosure of sunk cost, faithful migration to the latest confirmed contract and continuing debt reduction; implementation convenience does not justify pressuring the human to accept defects.

### Recording a design revision

- **Assess the change from the confirmed baseline.** Compare the cumulative changes in value, goals, assumptions, scope, invariants, trade-offs, interfaces, acceptance and downstream effects. Qualification requires a human-confirmed value path, explicit propositions and relationships, covered revision and scope, invariants, non-goals, acceptance and valid continuity. Dependencies, shipped code, agent summaries, silence and general approval cannot establish it.
- **Update the three records together.** Near the top of every new or revised design put a compact **Design authority** block identifying its registry entry, revision and sections, parent concepts and relationships, value rationale, invariants, non-goals, acceptance, confirmed baseline and continuity. In the registry, maintain separate concepts and edges with endpoints, kind, rationale, scoped version and status. Update exclusions, open questions, affected descendants, dependencies, reverse links and supersession. Invalidate only affected scope.
- **Preserve the decision.** Use a stable changelog ID and immutable commit/path locator. Record the human, explicit statement, source and time, covered revision, accepted or rejected scope and exclusions. Label excerpts when durable evidence is unavailable. Record corrections, withdrawals and supersession as new events. For missing evidence, describe the alternatives and decision needed. Register unqualified legacy documents as `pending-human` with the missing evidence and candidate value path.

<a id="5-evidence-access"></a>

### Evidence access

[Human decisions and agent continuity](../CHANGELOG.md#authority-evidence-index) are indexed by stable H/K ID in the changelog. Retrieve the referenced immutable source for exact propositions, scope, provenance or exclusions. Current status and lineage remain authoritative in this registry.

[Implementation fidelity](../CHANGELOG.md#h-implementation-correction-20261118) and the [scalar cost assessment](../CHANGELOG.md#k-scalar-correction-20261118) apply to the [scalar regression cases](ConstructionAndAcquisition.md#scalar-expression-checking-and-lowering).

### Current discussion queue

The design discussion owns the external working queue in `cod-project-worklogs/design-discussion-backlog-20261117.md`, under the workspace parent directory. A real program that exposes an unresolved observable choice takes priority. Record the question, governing contract and next decision trigger there. Mainline owns missing implementation evidence and cutover. The registry records each scope’s qualification and exclusions. [Review responsibilities](ImplementationArchitecture.md#design-review-and-delivery-responsibilities) · [Long-term design topics](../docs/DesignTopics.md)

| <a id="d-10-method-groups"></a>**D-10-METHOD-GROUPS** [Grouped method definitions and value dispatch](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#grouped-method-definitions-and-value-dispatch), D-10r270 / D-07r224 | **Confirmed** by the [named-constant decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-named-method-constants-20261125) and the [project user’s grouped-method decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-grouped-method-dispatch-20261125), P-740/P-741. Full shared signatures introduce method lists with `of`. Each method uses `=>`. A named declaration contributes methods to its selected family. A grouped installation activates a single complete local family. | Type and singleton-value refinements use unique-most-specific dispatch. Check a cross-module intersection, enum aliases, full installation coverage, retained owner/access and public argument labels. A direct-level parameterized trailing callback requires a braced method body. The planned formatter must preserve arm boundaries, callback attachment and control destinations at the 100-column limit. Check `=name` with a same-spelled formal and duplicate aliases or literals, including an enum code in its backing-typed slot. |
| <a id="d-10-family-effect-requirements"></a>**D-10-FAMILY-EFFECT-REQUIREMENTS** [Family-wide incoming symbols](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#family-wide-effect-requirements-before-20), r268 | The project user confirmed [family granularity](../CHANGELOG.md#h-family-effect-requirements-20261125), [branch-local supply](../CHANGELOG.md#h-booter-branch-local-effect-requirements-20261125) and [the seed removal and pre-2.0 checker scope](../CHANGELOG.md#h-effect-checking-cost-and-compiler-stages-20261125). The [capture and restoration decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-perform-family-capture-20261125) extends P-738/P-739 to handler execution. | The pre-2.0 booter and `e-wrks/cod` check the union of outstanding requirements from every branch and method in the selected family view. A successful local installation discharges the uses it supplies on that branch. Remove selected-path binding verification from the trusted seed. Keep the facts consumed by emission. Handler activation captures the same family union. Each `perform` replays every member and restores each prior binding, including on unwind. `behave`, dispatch, ownership and checked throws keep their contracts. Reconsider finer admission after 2.0. |
| <a id="d-10-actor-chan"></a>**D-10-ACTOR-CHAN** [Actor concurrency direction](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#actor-concurrency-direction), r242 scoped section | **Confirmed boundary** under [H-ACTOR-CHAN-BOUNDARY-20261123](../CHANGELOG.md#h-actor-chan-boundary-20261123), P-702, continuing H-151/H-168. | Actor and chan are the language terms. Each Actor has one dedicated OS thread, its own execution stack and isolated TLS bindings. Message types are statically checked. Sending is fire-and-forget except for reported systemic communication faults likely to compromise subsequent sends; no reception guarantee or receipt. Applications provide any further reliable protocols. The network region path and independent endpoint requirement follow [region transport](#d-32-region-transport) and [chan ends](#d-32-chan-ends). Endpoint forms/authority, receiver count, queue resources/order/close, fault detection/error surface, exact send consumption and fault ownership, Actor/module lifecycle, native failure containment and wire compatibility remain open. [D-32](#d-32) records the research; this scope does not qualify those proposals or upgrade P-131–P-135. |

| <a id="d-10-varargs"></a>**D-10-VARARGS** [Variadic family formation and pack binding](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#runtime-array-expansion-and-one-variadic-parameter), r273 scoped section | [Indexed borrowed elements](../CHANGELOG.md#k-variadic-element-selection-20261123) retain the selected targets and their ordinary lifetime. [By-value forwarding](../CHANGELOG.md#k-value-pack-forwarding-20261123) applies the same preparation at each call. [Indexed by-value elements](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#indexed-by-value-elements) follow ordinary callee-local storage and qualifiers ([continuity](../CHANGELOG.md#k-variadic-local-values-20261123)). **Multiple expansions and their order confirmed** under [H-ORDERED-PACK-EXPANSIONS-20261123](../CHANGELOG.md#h-ordered-pack-expansions-20261123). **Family rule and one-pack direction confirmed** under [the mixed-family decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-selector-literals-and-pack-dispatch-20261126), H-258/H-449 and the empty-pack part of H-475. P-403/P-404. Overall value path and full operational scope remain pending-human. | Admit fixed methods and one variadic member. Reject two distinct variadic members when their families combine. An explicit expansion selects the variadic route independently of runtime length. Deduplicate by defining method. Matching declaration and implementation count once. An applicable exact-count fixed method takes priority over implicit pack collection. Preserve element type, owner, access, initialization and contextual empty-pack evidence. Each expansion supplies the same tail. Finish its elements in index order before preparing the next operand. Failure skips later operands and entry. Indexed writes to writable by-value elements affect only the callee’s local values. Indexing a borrowed element selects its prepared target even after a source-slot rebind. Whole-pack escape, cross-signature adaptation and representation retain separate scope. |
| <a id="d-10-arguments"></a>**D-10-ARGUMENTS** [Named arguments, defaults and parameter initialization](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#named-arguments-defaults-and-complete-binding), r247 scoped section | [Different default origins at a function join](../CHANGELOG.md#h-inferred-function-defaults-20261123). **Local binding/preamble/context/syntax directions, phase order and named default origin and compatible targets, type-bound defaults and whole-family coverage confirmed H-451–H-462 and default-count ranking H-474 and explicit disambiguation H-475 and default type evidence/analysis order H-476/H-477; complete operational/value-path qualification pending-human.** P-405 through C-10/C-57 records candidate value; P-406 records dependencies. | [H-CALL-INTENT-20261119](../CHANGELOG.md#h-call-intent-20261119) confirms explicit disambiguation and omission of redundant labels. Positional values may follow an in-position label. An out-of-order label starts the named-only suffix; owner-projection arguments use explicit grouping. Used defaults run per omission or explicit request in definition or caller environments; caller-defaults introduce a scope. Symbol-only dependencies are recommended, with identity resolved at definition and value read from caller-current binding. Ordinary caller locals remain allowed. H-458 fixes explicit preparation, caller-default preparation, entry and callee-preamble defaults in that order. H-459 permits only named callable declarations to author defaults; parameter callable types may inherit them through typeof, retaining the original definition environment. H-460 permits ordinary compatible targets with established default dependencies. H-461 assigns the default expression/context association to the type contract rather than an additional value capture. H-462 requires every selected origin signature, with caller-local named adapters permitted. H-474 ranks ordinary type matches before fewer used defaults and rejects remaining ties; defaults execute only after selection. H-475/K-EXPLICIT-DEFAULT-SELECTION-20261115 governs explicit slot requests. `-> parameter` requests the declared default and `[]...` selects explicit pack form with established element/owner evidence. Requests count as used defaults and retain existing named binding, origin, phase and cleanup rules. H-476/H-477 permits established types of used defaults to complete generic inference only after unique overload selection, without execution, preserving supplied-input constraints, environments, origin and evaluation phases; unresolved/circular evidence rejects. Default contents cannot decide candidate applicability or resolve ambiguity; selected-default errors reject without retry. An inferred conditional with different default origins drops those defaults from its common function signature. [Grouped source definitions](#d-10-method-groups) supply the local adapter syntax. Physical context evidence and detailed inference procedures remain under review. |
| <a id="d-10-isa"></a>**D-10-ISA** [Type membership and branch refinement](SymbolicDynamicScope.md#type-membership-and-branch-refinement), D-10r205 | **Source contract confirmed**, [H-ISA-20261118](../CHANGELOG.md#h-isa-20261118). | <a id="d-17-catch-wrappers"></a>**D-17-CATCH-WRAPPERS** [Wrapper-aware catch matching](ExceptionControl.md#wrapper-aware-catch-matching), D-17r97 | **Transparent standard-wrapper matching, source order and catch-local same-identity tip selection confirmed**, H-518–H-522. H-523 requires same-checkedness causes, rejects incompatible fixed wrapper choices with a rethrow from e suggestion, and adds that automatically selected wrapper form. [H-CATCH-TIP-20261118](../CHANGELOG.md#h-catch-tip-20261118) selects the intrinsic readonly `e.tip` on the catch binding and reserves that field name on exception types. H-CAUSE-PTR-TIP-REF-20261118 selects a readonly non-null ref to the incoming Exception, with ordinary lifetime checks. The immediate cause uses the nullable ptr mode. Stable identity/owner implementation, additional inspection/bypass APIs and the checker proof for correlated coverage remain separate. Source support is unimplemented. |

[H-EXCEPTION-DECL-OWNED-20261118](../CHANGELOG.md#h-exception-decl-owned-20261118) makes both exception declaration spellings owning for locals and parameters. Ordinary ref declarations borrow. | <a id="d-20-bootstrap"></a>**D-20-BOOTSTRAP** [Retiring the reference commands](ImplementationArchitecture.md#retiring-a-replaced-compiler-pipeline) at roadmap r50. [Booter pipeline migration from the seed-built reference](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#replacing-the-booter-pipeline) at roadmap r49. [Fast compilation and default booter binary](#d-20-compiler-modes) at roadmap r45, [replacement pipeline](ImplementationArchitecture.md#replacement-seed-pipeline) and [stage controls](#d-20-stage-controls) at roadmap r44, [seed scope and pre-2.0 symbol requirements](#d-10-family-effect-requirements) at roadmap r43, [bootstrap executable and entry names](../applications/cod-booter/README.md#bootstrap-executable-and-entry-names) at roadmap r39, [trusted-source translation](../applications/cod-booter/README.md#trusted-source-and-the-seed) at roadmap r38, [initial seed delivery](../applications/cod-booter/README.md#initial-seed-delivery) at r19, [catch syntax](#d-17-exception-bindings) / [lexical names](#d-10-contextual-names) at r22, and [compiler memory acceptance](../applications/cod-booter/README.md#compiler-memory-acceptance) at roadmap r34 | **Trusted seed confirmed**, [H-TRUSTED-SEED-TRANSLATION-20261125](../CHANGELOG.md#h-trusted-seed-translation-20261125), P-642/P-643. [Workload-based build concurrency](../CHANGELOG.md#h-workload-based-build-concurrency-20261124) continues P-642/P-643. [63-file release observation](../CHANGELOG.md#k-booter-entry-world-release-20261124). [Measured fixture budgets](../CHANGELOG.md#k-booter-memory-baseline-20261124) continue the r31 scope. [Seed maintainability, bounded concurrency and booter RAM](../CHANGELOG.md#h-bootstrap-compiler-memory-20261124). **Delivery scope and source-readability requirement confirmed**, [H-BOOTER-DRIVEN-SEED-20261119](../CHANGELOG.md#h-booter-driven-seed-20261119). **Required native scope confirmed**, [H-BOOTER-NATIVE-MINIMUM-20261119](../CHANGELOG.md#h-booter-native-minimum-20261119), P-642/P-643. | The C++ seed emits LLVM from trusted CoD. Faithful translation of the booter sets its scope. General static soundness belongs to the CoD booter. Additional seed checks need a concrete engineering use and explicit opt-in. The seed-built booter must exercise the shared C interface, basic in-memory C region support and minimal C-facing LLVM verification and native emission. Demonstrate representative output, resource lifetimes, required exception handling and direct invalid-input reporting. [D-20-C-PROVIDERS](#d-20-c-providers) governs generation and [D-20-C-HANDLES](#d-20-c-handles) governs native resource ownership. [D-20-DIAGNOSTICS](#d-20-diagnostics) governs the booter’s diagnostic scope. Business application and research paper prototypes belong to separate work. Admitted programs follow existing contracts. |
| <a id="d-20-repositories"></a>**D-20-REPOSITORIES** [Production `cfe` milestones](#d-20-compiler-modes), [production compiler stage controls](#d-20-stage-controls), [next-stage repositories and native runtime](../applications/cod-booter/README.md#next-stage-repositories-and-native-runtime), roadmap r37 | **Repository roles and GitHub organization confirmed for the planned split**, [H-LANG-REPOSITORY-20261119](../CHANGELOG.md#h-lang-repository-20261119) and [H-TOOLCHAIN-REPOSITORIES-20261118](../CHANGELOG.md#h-toolchain-repositories-20261118). The five compiler and toolchain repositories belong under `e-wrks`. The [manual bootstrap packages](../CHANGELOG.md#h-manual-bootstrap-packages-20261125) decision assigns future package management to `e-wrks/cod`. `cod-lang` owns language design and the shared specification. `cod-seed` retains the CoD-written booter, C++ seed and C native components under [H-BOOTER-EXCEPTIONS-DIAGNOSTICS-20261120](../CHANGELOG.md#h-booter-exceptions-diagnostics-20261120). `cod` hosts the production compiler and its full diagnostics. `codup` manages toolchain versions and their [production LLVM and target components](#d-20-target-toolchain) at roadmap r52. `codmate` provides the LSP server and IDE plugins. | Existing C-80/P-314–P-318 scope and candidate P-586. The C region core will need target VM and ownership protocols. The [native C interface](#d-20-native-c) is selected. The [production parser bundle](#d-20-parser-bundle) specifies two distribution artifacts. Repository migration, other distribution details and the detailed ABI remain deferred. The [project user's parser decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-production-parser-direction-20261124) assigns the CoD parser-combinator library to the production compiler. Review a booter backport after the production implementation is complete. The [production parser plan](../docs/DesignTopics.md#production-parser-combinators) records the comparison on the common grammar. See the [library support WIP](#d-parser-combinator-research). |
| <a id="d-20-target-toolchain"></a>**D-20-TARGET-TOOLCHAIN** [Production cross-builds and target runtimes](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#production-cross-builds-and-target-runtimes), pipeline r13 and roadmap r52 | **Confirmed** by [H-PRODUCTION-CROSS-BUILD](../CHANGELOG.md#h-production-cross-build-20261126), C-80/P-754/P-755. `codup` distributes the required LLVM components. Hosted targets permit a compatible CRT for C packages. Static musl is preferred for self-contained Linux delivery. Freestanding SoC targets can supply their own runtime. | Host execution and optimized artifact defaults follow [D-20-COMPILER-MODES](#d-20-compiler-modes). Initial targets, linker packaging, board startup and backing acquisition are open. Implementation acceptance belongs to the production toolchain. |
| <a id="d-20-compiler-modes"></a>**D-20-COMPILER-MODES** [Running with `cod` and building with `cfe`](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#default-execution-and-optimized-builds), pipeline r13, roadmap r52 | **Confirmed** by the [host and cross-build decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-production-cross-build-20261126), the [single booter mode decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-booter-single-codegen-mode-20261126), the [production CLI decision](../CHANGELOG.md#h-cfe-output-directory-20261126) and [H-DEFAULT-RUN-AND-OPTIMIZED-BUILD-20261125](../CHANGELOG.md#h-default-run-and-optimized-build-20261125), C-80/P-746/P-747. [Editorial continuity](../CHANGELOG.md#k-cfe-entry-point-20261126) · [CLI naming](../CHANGELOG.md#k-cod-cli-name-20261126). | The `cod` command (Compile on Demand) builds and runs on the host and leaves no executable for the user. Only `cfe` offers cross-builds. The seed defaults to producing the `cod-booter` binary. Seed, booter and first-release production target Go-like fast compilation and moderate runtime performance. The booter ships one code-generation mode. User-selectable optimization trade-offs belong to `cfe`. The production `cfe` (Compile for Ever) command saves the optimized executable in `~/.local/bin/` by default, with a directory override in `~/.cod/config.yaml`. The user can run it after the build. Experimental in the first production release, formal in 2.0. Backend settings, flag spellings and numeric thresholds are open. |
| <a id="d-20-stage-controls"></a>**D-20-STAGE-CONTROLS** [Independently selectable compiler operations](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#independently-selectable-compiler-operations), pipeline r7; [required internal phase boundaries](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#replacing-the-booter-pipeline) at r10, continued at r14 for [demand-directed initialization readiness](../CHANGELOG.md#k-booter-demand-readiness-20261126), r15 for [shared enum assertion selection](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#enum-assertions-in-source-and-object-commands) and r16 for [ordinary enum overload selection](../applications/cod-booter/docs/CompilerPipeline.md#ordinary-overloads-on-an-enum-and-its-backing-type) | **Confirmed** by [H-COMPILER-PIPELINE-REPLACEMENT-20261125](../CHANGELOG.md#h-compiler-pipeline-replacement-20261125), C-80/P-744/P-745. | Independent parse, type, lexical-access, ownership and emit controls are a design goal for the replacement seed and subsequently the booter, and a delivery requirement for production LSP/IDE and builds. Preserve prerequisite identity, observable execution and the distinction between skipped and successful checks. Protocol and persistence details remain open. |

| <a id="d-30-typed-subscripts"></a>**D-30-TYPED-SUBSCRIPTS** [Multidimensional subscript overloads](CollectionsAndBuiltinContainers.md#multidimensional-subscript-overloads), D-30r9 / D-11r40 / D-14r138 | **Confirmed** under the user's [requested refinement](../CHANGELOG.md#h-ranges-and-typed-subscripts-20261126), P-766/P-767. | [RHS before index parameters](../CHANGELOG.md#h-selector-literals-and-pack-dispatch-20261126) permits a final pack. Unit-bearing axes preserve their identities, with explicit library overloads for ordinal or measured advances. Library contracts govern result layout, views and bounds. |

Exact ABI, platform protocols, migration and implementation qualification remain open. |

### Current supersession and audit boundaries

**Init source clarification (H-601/H-602):** D-22-INIT-PLACES replaces the pending D-22-INIT-TARGET draft; P-555–P-557 now records the current ordinary-reference/explicit-var contract. No target modifier is selected. The earlier slot-only interpretation and its overly broad H-571 attribution are corrected by the new changelog event, without rewriting historical evidence. D-22 general result destinations, D-28 initialization flow and D-20 native planning retain their independent qualification boundaries.

**Source compiler restructuring (H-399):** on `main`, historical implementation evidence in D-07/D-10/D-15/D-17/D-18 and the source fixtures describes the preserved `multi-dispatch` baseline, not current feature availability. The source foundation supports checked scalar methods, direct AOT specializations, shared general bodies, recursion, lexical scalar storage, nullable local views, scalar borrows with cross-call initialization and returned-target summaries, complete nested callable values with structural generic signatures, eager operator methods with checked builtin delegation, bounded intrinsic exceptions and prepared/deferred cleanup with recovery. Regional, abstract-input, general callable capture, richer relational proofs and symbolic import/general module-composition paths remain explicit implementation gaps. Source-preserving file selections retain isolated names/contexts, explicit publication and once-only encounter-ordered initialization. Ordinary import retains source definitions and signatures through receiver interpretations, shared aliases/extensions and inherited-overload conflict checks. Confirmed language contracts remain the target. [The source capability reference](https://github.com/complyue/cod-project/blob/b7fd6327984f537e2256ca14383bbeedd7e833bd/tests/cod/language/README.md) describes current supported/missing behavior.

These are scoped replacements, not blanket invalidations. Apply them to older wording; unaffected requirements remain. Detailed originals remain in H/K evidence.

| Scope | Current decision / evidence | Affected contracts |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capture/function effects | H-180/H-181 preserves captured values/hidden owners and forbids dynamic-owner extraction; H-197/H-198 replaces readonly function snapshots with explicit-memory access. | D-10/D-13/D-14/D-18 |
| Composition/publication | H-212 assigns redefinition to mixin; H-218/H-220 requires explicit conformance and distinguishes adapter import from consumer visibility; H-230–H-233 separates identities/aliases and requires publication; H-245 forbids foreign-world editing. | D-07/D-10/D-12/D-15 |
| Static reference owners | H-417 requires exactly one statically provable owner for each ref/ptr; omitted annotations infer from initialization and non-unique/unknown owner evidence rejects. Owner proof is required at initialization, including for null views. It preserves runtime target choice within one owner and per-call symbolic owner instantiation. | D-14r87/D-18r91/D-23r9; D-10r147/D-24r7 dependencies; concrete-type evidence remains separate |
| Null owner syntax | H-418 requires an owner on the receiving declaration or null literal; bare ptr p:T = null rejects. H-419 withdraws outer syntax; here selects the current full lexical scope and a named region/generic owner uses existing owner proof. An enclosing variable carries its owner inward through =, including null views. Both explicit assignment/initialization sides must agree. H-440 uses is null; its bare null gets the comparison operand’s owner context, while explicit different-owner identity comparisons do not join reference owners. | D-14r99/D-10r150/D-23r13; P-282; no allocation, target read or lifetime extension |

D-23 whole-file review remains. | D-14r87/D-23r9; D-24r7 dependency; D-17/D-18 contracts preserved |
| Parameter storage | H-271/H-272 gives callee-local default targets; H-273–H-276 specifies explicit owner/region forms. Earlier external-borrow defaults are not universal. | D-10/D-14/D-17/D-18 |

Voluntary throws declarations retain the existing fixed caller obligation. | Generic operator contracts | H-402 permits direct generic operation use under ordinary applicability rules. Known gaps remain scoped above; shortening their records needs no new approval. Current contract revisions and qualification are maintained in the scope table.

**Type-associated declaration lookup:** H-483/D-15-TYPE-METHODS replaces H-226 only where a local definition previously extended a family implicitly selected by import of a type. Both type reuse forms now allow local shadowing. Explicit callable imports, source identities, H-232 existing explicit adaptation and H-253 conflict checks continue; a local shadow with an independent identity does not supply a source-helper implementation by spelling alone; explicit family selection retains its existing connection.

Node descriptions identify topics, not blanket approval. Relationship rows carry rationale/status; §2 carries current document qualification. Contract links support review in either direction through edge endpoints, without adding value or dependency edges. Inspect all edges naming a changed node, then both endpoints and their contracts; dependency cycles cannot prove value.

| <a id="c-24"></a>C-24 | 个体神经元的离散时间 rate／有效活动动力学与自然传播终止：保留学习连接，活动可因支持不足或随机失败而停止；半衰期式目标宽松且 token 间可不同。 | [D-08](#d-08) |
| <a id="c-25"></a>C-25 | 分数式神经元选取／参与：以可分贡献表达训练目标，与 rate 协作缓解整细胞计数的语义割裂。 | [D-08](#d-08) |
| <a id="c-26"></a>C-26 | 背景支持下的有用小循环上下文：大链构建避免主动制造有害捷径，同时保留能让较早输入影响后续输出的小循环。 | [D-08](#d-08) |
| <a id="c-27"></a>C-27 | 累积／延后生效的衰减：保留跨小更新的衰减进度，可研究下一数值边界的未来触发。 | [D-08](#d-08) |
| <a id="c-28"></a>C-28 | 按建模量选择 INT8/INT4、无符号／定点或浮点表示，分别论证存储、缩放、累加与动态更新精度。 | [D-08](#d-08) |

## 4. Relationship register

Each row preserves a distinct proposition, endpoints, relation kind, rationale and scope. Historical revisions identify evidence coverage; they do not override §2 or qualify later content. An unspecified formal value path remains pending-human. Local confirmations retain their stated extent; P-31 is rejected. Dependencies never grant authority.

| P-680 | D-20-MODULE-GRAPH derives-from | C-80 | H-COMPILER-LAYERS-20261122 delegates the first-booter architecture. Scoped graph r34 covers the CoD nominal sum and graph-region parsers, constructor field reception in direct case arms, typed owning region result clauses, declaration-leading const on the budget local, named reference-slot, fixed value and fixed reference parameters in the visit, checked constructor interfaces and selection, finite aggregate schema classification, typed consumers, the isolated object route and the first regional edge, including array transport, path-key consumption, parsing the dependency symbol’s procedure type and scoped definition, per-visit activation, local handler writes to graph table holders, the CoD module index, distinct builtin and graph-only export kind codes in the shared selection row, builtin and dependency selection, visit publication, LLVM construction and provider generation using selected-target ABI facts and shared reserved member spellings, standalone native membership, frontend proof for the path wrapper’s constructing throw and its private owning completion through cleanup, cross-unit exception tags, field offsets and retained canonical and occurrence-specific diagnostic names, and the ready path’s validated lexical here call and nominal feedback. Each typed command receives its fresh graph from one CoD factory under the direct owning-result scope. A synchronous terminal borrows published ranges under an ordinary input owner and retains access to its command’s destination. |

R175 requires design confirmation for new or materially revised architecture and algorithms. The [r132 seed review](ImplementationArchitecture.md#first-seed-call-with-completed-behavior) covers the first closed scalar procedure. The four-callback interface and regional representation of open rules retain their research status. |
| P-750 | D-EPS-RESEARCH derives-from | C-97 / C-52 | Candidate value path at r1. Reusing domain stages across inputs, terminals and deployments should preserve each drive's control and each Actor's local authority. The [commission](../CHANGELOG.md#h-eps-library-direction-20261126) confirms the product and research direction. |
| P-751 | D-EPS-RESEARCH depends-on | D-31-COMPOSITE / D-29-GENERALIZED-FOR / D-18-HERE-STORAGE / D-14 / D-32-CHAN-ENDS / D-32-REGION-TRANSPORT | Scoped dependencies at r1. Local feedback and cleanup follow the first two contracts, retained policies and callbacks require actual owners, and the inter-Actor cases take their send, reception and validation boundaries from chan. Timeline sampling and wider EPS interfaces remain under investigation. |
| P-752 | D-DBMR-SERVICE-RESEARCH derives-from | C-03 / C-05 / C-09 | Candidate value path for the initial paper and guide. Sharing a large indexed ontology across applications and keeping frequent writes in small databases may reduce lifetime engineering and operating costs. Draft 2 includes externally maintained CoD packages. Compare per-deployment adoption and operations costs under equivalent guarantees and account for shared component maintenance once across adopters. |
| P-753 | D-DBMR-SERVICE-RESEARCH depends-on | D-07 / D-14 / D-22-DBMR-ACCESS / D-32-REGION-TRANSPORT | Independent owners govern packet, catalog, writable state and result. Published images retain their immutable contract. The domain service defines concurrent updates, durable outcomes and replay. Specialized writable reopening and the execution service require further design. |
| P-754 | D-20-TARGET-TOOLCHAIN derives-from | C-80 / charter §§1, 3.2 and 6.1 | Pipeline r13. A managed toolchain can deploy a program and its C components to the selected execution environment without requiring a system LLVM installation. A board can supply the memory and output services it actually has. Confirmed by the project user. |
| P-755 | D-20-TARGET-TOOLCHAIN depends-on | D-20-COMPILER-MODES / D-20-REPOSITORIES / D-20-NATIVE-C / D-14 | At pipeline r13 and roadmap r52, command selection fixes the host or target. Native parts share its ABI. Changing region backing must preserve alignment, capacity and owner/release pairing. |
| P-756 | D-JULIA-PKG-RESEARCH derives-from | C-10 / C-65 / charter §§1, 4.3 and 8 | Candidate value path at r1. Reusing typed array and numerical operations should lower the number of translation decisions while preserving the result and its owner. The project user confirmed the porting goal and preference for compatible Julia semantics. |
| P-757 | D-JULIA-PKG-RESEARCH depends-on | D-10-TYPED-EXPR / D-11 / D-20-C-PROVIDERS / D-20-C-DECLARATIONS / D-14 / D-30 | At r1, the conversion and diagnostic baseline governs the proposed integer ties. Provider results, regional access and array traversal govern the statistical and distance workloads. Specific replacement semantics and delivery require separate qualification. |
| P-758 | D-30-ARRAY-POLICY derives-from | C-10 / C-65 / charter §§1, 4.3 and 8 | Confirmed r2. An author selects the coordinate and storage conventions their algorithm uses and can review actual native loans at the boundary. |
| P-759 | D-30-ARRAY-POLICY depends-on | D-30 / D-14 / D-15 / D-20-C-PROVIDERS | Confirmed r2. Visible library identity, owner/access checks and provider ABI constrain views and writable native interoperation. |
| P-760 | D-10-LIBRARY-FIXITY derives-from | C-65 / charter §§3.3 and 8 | Confirmed r2. A domain library can publish a recognizable expression vocabulary while source grouping and effects remain reviewable. |
| P-761 | D-10-LIBRARY-FIXITY depends-on | D-10-OPERATOR-RUNS / D-15 / D-10-TYPED-EXPR | Confirmed r2. Stable tokens and visible declarations determine grouping before type dispatch. CoD assertions retain their grammar across imports. |
| P-762 | D-07-TYPE-SYNONYMS derives-from | C-10 / C-65 / charter §§1 and 8 | Confirmed r2. Library authors can map established type names with one declaration while preserving the identity used to review a port. |
| P-763 | D-07-TYPE-SYNONYMS depends-on | D-07 / D-11 / D-15 / D-20-C-PROVIDERS | Confirmed r2. Canonical identities, signedness, module visibility and native representation determine interchangeability. |
| P-764 | D-29-STEPPED-RANGES derives-from | C-10 / C-65 / charter §§1 and 8 | Confirmed r25. A range has one inspectable progression shared by algorithms and array users. |
